# MARION-IA-USA General Proxmox Maintenance Plan — V2

**Scope:** General maintenance across all Proxmox nodes  
**Execution host:** VM/LXC 906 `hermes-jordan` on `MIAM-00100`  
**Mode:** Controlled maintenance, one node at a time  
**Out of scope:** RDMA/MTU/network optimization, PBS redesign, kernel upgrades, firmware upgrades, NVIDIA/CUDA changes, Mellanox/ConnectX/RDMA package changes, hostname changes

---

## 1. Objectives

1. Bring safe Debian/Proxmox userspace packages up to date.
2. Reboot nodes one at a time where appropriate, except `MIAM-00100`, which will be rebooted manually after the agent finishes.
3. Verify each node returns healthy before continuing.
4. Deeply investigate the known hardware-error condition on `MIAM-00112`.
5. Trace corrected PCIe/AER events on GPU hosts without changing PCIe, BIOS, VFIO, NVIDIA, or RDMA configuration.
6. Leave hostname naming inconsistencies unchanged.
7. Leave PBS and backup architecture unchanged.
8. Produce one final Markdown handoff with everything changed, deferred, and still requiring attention.

---

## 2. Cluster in Scope

Current 16-node cluster:

- MIAM-00100
- MIAM-00110
- MIAM-00111
- MIAM-00112
- MIAM-00113
- MIAM-00114
- MIAM-00115
- MIAM-00117
- MIAM-00118
- MIAM-00119
- MIAM-00133
- MIAM-00135
- MIAM-00143
- MIAM-00144
- MIAM-00147
- MIAM-00149

Do not rename any host, alter `/etc/hostname`, or change cluster node names.

---

## 3. Hard Safety Rules

Do **not**:

- change hostnames
- change bridges, VLANs, bonds, routes, gateways, MTU, or switch settings
- run RDMA tuning or benchmarking
- run `ib_write_bw`, `ib_read_bw`, or RDMA stress tests
- change ConnectX/Mellanox configuration
- change VFIO mappings or PCI passthrough
- change BIOS settings
- upgrade/install a new kernel
- update firmware or microcode
- update NVIDIA drivers, CUDA, or NVIDIA container/runtime packages
- update Mellanox/OFED/RDMA packages
- redesign or configure PBS
- alter backup retention/schedules in this project
- run `apt dist-upgrade` or an unrestricted `apt full-upgrade`
- reboot multiple Proxmox nodes at once
- reboot `MIAM-00100` automatically

If an action crosses one of these boundaries:

> **STOP → DOCUMENT → DEFER TO THE APPROPRIATE PROJECT**

---

## 4. Accepted Conditions

Record but do **not** change:

- Existing hostname formatting inconsistencies.
- Intentionally unsubscribed Proxmox hosts.
- Existing backup architecture and retention until the separate PBS project.
- Existing RDMA/MTU configuration until the separate RDMA project.
- LXC nesting settings unless a production container is actually failing because of them.

For repositories, verify the appropriate Proxmox no-subscription repositories are configured. Do not treat absence of a subscription key as a failure.

---

## 5. Required Preflight

From VM/LXC 906:

1. Confirm all 16 cluster nodes are online.
2. Confirm quorum.
3. Capture current package/kernel state.
4. Capture running QEMU VMs and LXCs using **SSH on each node**, not only the API.
5. Capture storage health.
6. Capture kernel/hardware errors.
7. Confirm 906 is still running on `MIAM-00100`.

Run centrally where appropriate:

```bash
pvecm status
pvecm nodes
```

On every node:

```bash
hostname
pveversion -v
uname -r
qm list
pct list
pvesm status
df -h
systemctl --failed --no-pager
journalctl -p err..alert -b --no-pager
```

If quorum is unhealthy, expected storage is unavailable, or a node has an unexplained critical hardware/storage fault:

> Stop maintenance and document the blocker.

---

## 6. Package Update Policy

### 6.1 Refresh metadata

```bash
apt update
apt list --upgradable 2>/dev/null
dpkg --audit
```

### 6.2 Simulate before installing

Do not blindly upgrade everything.

Explicitly exclude packages matching or related to:

```text
kernel
pve-kernel
proxmox-kernel
linux-image
linux-headers
firmware
microcode
nvidia
cuda
libcuda
nvml
mellanox
mlx
rdma
infiniband
ibverbs
ofed
```

Also defer any package whose dependency chain would install/remove/replace one of the above.

### 6.3 Safe userspace updates

The agent may install ordinary Debian/Proxmox userspace updates only when the simulated transaction shows:

- no kernel replacement
- no firmware/microcode update
- no NVIDIA/CUDA change
- no Mellanox/RDMA change
- no removal of Proxmox packages
- no unexpected storage/network package replacement
- no dependency conflict

Prefer an explicit package list over unrestricted `full-upgrade`.

Before:

```bash
dpkg-query -W -f='${Package}	${Version}
' > /root/packages-before-maintenance.txt
```

After:

```bash
dpkg-query -W -f='${Package}	${Version}
' > /root/packages-after-maintenance.txt
```

Record the exact packages changed.

---

## 7. Reboot Strategy

Process **one host at a time**.

For every host selected for reboot:

1. Verify cluster quorum.
2. Inventory running guests with `qm list` and `pct list`.
3. Gracefully stop or safely drain workloads on that host.
4. For GPU/model-serving hosts, use the LLM Manager to remove the node from active inference before reboot.
5. Verify an alternate inference path is available before taking model-serving nodes down.
6. Reboot only that host.
7. Wait for it to return.
8. Verify Proxmox services, cluster membership, storage, networking, and guests.
9. Only then continue.

Post-reboot:

```bash
hostname
uptime
pveversion -v
uname -r
pvecm status
pvesm status
qm list
pct list
systemctl --failed --no-pager
journalctl -p err..alert -b --no-pager
```

### Kernel rule

A reboot must **not intentionally switch the host to a newly installed kernel** during this project.

If a new kernel is staged unexpectedly, do not reboot that host until reviewed and documented.

---

## 8. Recommended Maintenance Order

### Phase A — Low-risk / lightly loaded nodes

```text
MIAM-00110
MIAM-00114
MIAM-00115
MIAM-00117
MIAM-00118
MIAM-00119
MIAM-00149
MIAM-00113
```

Confirm current workloads immediately before reboot.

### Phase B — Infrastructure/service nodes

```text
MIAM-00147
MIAM-00133
MIAM-00135
```

Special care:

- `MIAM-00133` may carry remote-access and infrastructure services.
- `MIAM-00135` hosts important LLM/agent-management services.
- `MIAM-00147` contains backup/storage services.

Verify service impact and recovery/access paths before rebooting.

### Phase C — GPU / AI nodes

```text
MIAM-00112
MIAM-00111
MIAM-00143
MIAM-00144
```

Before each reboot:

- drain local inference from that node
- verify the LLM Manager is not routing new work to it
- verify fallback inference is available
- do not modify NVIDIA, VFIO, PCIe, ConnectX, RDMA, or MTU configuration

### Phase D — MIAM-00100

`MIAM-00100` hosts Jordan Hermes 906.

The agent may:

- inspect it
- perform safe userspace package updates
- determine whether a reboot is recommended
- prepare exact reboot/verification instructions

The agent must **not reboot MIAM-00100**.

A human will manually reboot it after the maintenance run is complete.

---

## 9. MIAM-00112 Deep Hardware Investigation

Because of the known bent CPU-socket pins and corrected hardware/MCE events, perform deeper read-only diagnostics.

Do not change BIOS, reseat hardware, or alter memory settings in this plan.

Run:

```bash
journalctl -k --no-pager | grep -Ei 'mce|machine check|hardware error|edac|ecc|corrected|uncorrected'
dmesg -T | grep -Ei 'mce|machine check|hardware error|edac|ecc|corrected|uncorrected'
lscpu -e
dmidecode -t processor
dmidecode -t memory
```

If present:

```bash
find /sys/devices/system/edac -maxdepth 4 -type f -readable -print
```

Determine as far as evidence allows:

- CPU/socket associated with events
- memory channel/DIMM association
- corrected versus uncorrectable
- whether counts continue increasing
- whether workload or temperature appears correlated
- whether the host is stable enough for continued use

Classify:

```text
MONITOR
MAINTENANCE RECOMMENDED
HARDWARE SERVICE REQUIRED
REMOVE FROM PRODUCTION
```

Do not automatically remove the node unless uncorrectable/fatal errors or clear instability are observed.

---

## 10. PCIe/AER Investigation on GPU Nodes

Inspect read-only on:

```text
MIAM-00111
MIAM-00112
MIAM-00143
MIAM-00144
```

Run:

```bash
journalctl -k --no-pager | grep -Ei 'AER|PCIe Bus Error|pcieport|hardware error'
dmesg -T | grep -Ei 'AER|PCIe Bus Error|pcieport'
lspci -tv
lspci -nn
```

For any BDF mentioned:

```bash
lspci -vv -s <BDF>
```

Map:

- root port
- downstream device
- GPU/NIC/bridge if identifiable
- corrected vs uncorrectable severity
- recurrence count
- first/last observed timestamp

Do not change PCIe generation, ASPM, ACS, Above-4G decoding, ReBAR, IOMMU, VFIO, BIOS, or passthrough.

If only corrected events are present and the node is stable, document and monitor.

If uncorrectable/fatal events appear, stop maintenance on that node and escalate.

---

## 11. Storage Health Verification

Before and after maintenance:

```bash
lsblk -o NAME,SIZE,FSTYPE,MOUNTPOINTS,MODEL,SERIAL
df -h
pvesm status
```

Where ZFS exists:

```bash
zpool status
zpool list
zfs list
```

Where SMART/NVMe tools are already installed, inspect physical media.

Do not modify ZFS topology.

Any degraded pool, SMART failure, NVMe critical warning, or new I/O error is a stop condition.

---

## 12. Backup Policy During This Project

Do **not** configure PBS.

Do **not** alter backup scheduling or retention.

Leave the currently functioning native backup/restore path in place.

The separate PBS project will address the intended retention target:

```text
3 latest
14 daily
8 weekly
6 monthly
2 yearly
```

Move any backup-related observations into the PBS backlog.

---

## 13. RDMA / Networking Boundary

This plan must not attempt RDMA or MTU optimization.

If an RDMA, ConnectX, VFIO-network, jumbo-frame, or 100G issue is found:

```text
DEFER TO RDMA PLAN
```

Do not change MTU, guest/host RDMA interface configuration, Mellanox drivers, switch ports, or the RDMA ring.

---

## 14. Per-Node Completion Criteria

A node is complete only when:

- node online
- cluster sees node
- quorum healthy
- expected storage active
- no failed systemd service requiring investigation
- expected guests present
- expected guests restarted
- safe package maintenance complete
- prohibited package classes untouched
- no new critical hardware/storage errors
- before/after package state recorded
- reboot result recorded, if rebooted

If any check fails:

> Stop that node's maintenance, document the issue, and do not continue changing that node.

---

## 15. Final Validation

After all autonomous work except the MIAM-00100 reboot:

```bash
pvecm status
pvecm nodes
```

Verify across all nodes:

- online state
- quorum
- time sync
- storage state
- failed services
- guest inventory
- pending safe updates
- deferred kernel/firmware/NVIDIA/RDMA updates
- hardware warnings
- reboot status

Confirm:

- hostnames were not modified
- PBS was not modified
- RDMA/MTU configuration was not modified

---

## 16. Final Handoff File

Create:

```text
MARION-IA-USA-GENERAL-MAINTENANCE-HANDOFF-YYYYMMDD.md
```

Required sections:

### Executive Summary
- nodes maintained
- nodes rebooted
- nodes not rebooted
- overall cluster health

### Package Maintenance

For each node:

```text
Node:
Packages before:
Packages updated:
Packages deliberately excluded:
Packages still pending:
Reboot performed:
```

### MIAM-00112 Hardware Findings

Include exact error types, corrected/uncorrectable status, CPU/socket association, memory/DIMM association if available, current stability, and recommended physical action.

### PCIe/AER Findings

For each GPU host:

```text
Host:
Root port/device:
Error class:
Corrected/uncorrectable:
Count/recurrence:
Recommended action:
```

### Storage Health

Summarize SMART/NVMe/ZFS and capacity findings.

### Deferred — Kernel/Firmware

List relevant deferred updates.

### Deferred — NVIDIA/GPU Software

List deferred NVIDIA/CUDA items.

### Deferred — RDMA Project

List all network/RDMA/MTU items without fixing them.

### Deferred — PBS Project

List backup/PBS/scheduling/retention items without fixing them.

### MIAM-00100 Manual Reboot

Before reboot:

```bash
pvecm status
qm list
pct list
systemctl --failed --no-pager
```

Manual reboot:

```bash
reboot
```

After return:

```bash
uptime
pveversion -v
uname -r
pvecm status
pvesm status
qm list
pct list
systemctl --failed --no-pager
journalctl -p err..alert -b --no-pager
```

Verify `hermes-jordan` / 906 is running and reachable.

---

## 17. Agent Stop Conditions

Immediately stop and hand off if:

- cluster loses quorum
- more than one node becomes unexpectedly unavailable
- a node fails to return after reboot
- unexpected kernel upgrade is staged
- package transaction wants to remove Proxmox components
- package transaction touches NVIDIA/RDMA/firmware prohibited classes
- ZFS pool degrades
- SMART/NVMe critical error appears
- uncorrectable MCE/ECC error appears
- fatal PCIe/AER error appears
- remote management path becomes unreliable
- the agent can no longer confidently identify the executing host or protected workload

When uncertain:

> **STOP → PRESERVE STATE → DOCUMENT → HAND OFF**

---

## 18. Success Criteria

This project is complete when:

1. Safe userspace maintenance is complete across the cluster.
2. Nodes approved for autonomous reboot have been rebooted one at a time and verified.
3. MIAM-00100 is updated but left for manual reboot.
4. MIAM-00112 has a clear hardware-error diagnosis and recommendation.
5. PCIe/AER events on GPU hosts are traced and classified.
6. No hostname, RDMA, MTU, PBS, kernel, firmware, or NVIDIA configuration was changed.
7. A complete handoff file exists for follow-on work.

Never echo, never commit.\n- **Auth**: `POST /api2/json/access/ticket` with urlencoded\n  `username=<user>@LLDAP-Domain&password=...` → then send cookie `PVEAuthCookie` on every\n  call + header `CSRFPreventionToken` for non-GET. TLS is self-signed → ssl context with\n  `check_hostname=False, verify_mode=CERT_NONE`.\n- **Ready-made helper**: `scripts/pve_api.py` (login / realms / nodes / resources /\n  permissions / vmconfig <node> <vmid> / vmstatus <node> <vmid> / raw <METHOD> <path> [body];\n  also importable: `from pve_api import api, load_creds`).\n- **Node short-names are INCONSISTENT** — ring nodes are `miam00111/miam00112/miam00143/\n  miam00144` (no hyphen) while `miam-00135`, `miam-00100`, `miam-00149` carry hyphens.\n  NEVER guess a node name in an API path; enumerate `GET /api2/json/nodes` first.\n- **Guest exec via API** (replaces qm guest exec + most SSH-to-VM work):\n  `POST /nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec` with body\n  `{\"command\": [\"bash\", \"-lc\", \"echo <b64-of-script> | base64 -d | bash\"]}`, then poll\n  `GET .../agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` until `exited` (fields: exitcode, out-data,\n  err-data). **PITFALL: urlencode the body with `doseq=True`** — the `command` value is a\n  LIST, and plain urlencode silently DROPS list values, so the POST returns 200-ish while\n  the exec never runs (dispatch returns no pid / 596-looking failures).\n- **VM lifecycle** via `/nodes/<n>/qemu/<id>/status/start` (and stop/reboot) is clean and\n  audited by the bot identity; track completion via `/nodes/<n>/tasks/<upid>/status`.\n- **PDU power portal** (Jordan, 2026-09-11): web-managed PDU portal at\n  `https://10.0.20.154/` (nginx front; gunicorn binds 127.0.0.1:5000 — :5000 from outside\n  is refused; `http://` 301s to https). Creds staged in `~/.pdu_portal` (600).\n  **Login quirks:** the LLDAP username/password form REJECTED the staged root creds\n  (2026-09-11); the **emergency local administrator** form works — POST `/login` with\n  `mode=emergency`, `e_user`, `e_pass`. The post-login redirect Location is RELATIVE\n  (`/`) — resolve against the base URL or requests/urllib throws MissingSchema. The\n  first dashboard GET can exceed 10s; use a 30s timeout. Outlet rows carry `data-ip`,\n  `data-outlet`, and a label div (153/7 = MIAM-00147 NUC, 153/8 = MIAM-00148 ThunderBay)\n  — verify live labels before cycling. Control-plane only — power telemetry polling\n  stays on Emporia/GPU NVML per the standing rule (never poll PDU network endpoints\n  .151-.153 for telemetry).\n\n### PVE host/guest shell without sshpass (pexpect) + cloud-init & serial recipes\n\nWhen you must drive Proxmox hosts/VMs from a LAN Hermes box (no web GUI, no existing\ninjected key for the host, possibly no DHCP on the segment, optionally offline hosts),\nthe reusable recipes live in `references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md`, and a ready-made\ndrain-safe password-SSH script runner is `scripts/ssh_run.py` (runs a local .sh on a\nnode, saves output; reads the credential from the profile memory file at runtime and\nnever prints it). Key points:\n- pexpect (user-level `~/ssh-venv`) replaces missing `sshpass`; key-based variant for VMs.\n  Ready-made drain-loop runner: `scripts/ssh_run.py` (see scripts dir; handles the\n  empty/truncated `child.before` pitfall and reads the password from profile memory).\n  **Password auth REQUIRES pexpect** — `subprocess.run(..., input=PW)` fails silently\n  (Permission denied) because the password prompt appears AFTER stdin is consumed.\n  If `sshpass` is absent and uninstalled, port the loop to pexpect, never to input-feed.\n- ssh_run.py takes **IP addresses or full resolvable names, not bare node short-names**\n  (`miam00112` → \"Could not resolve hostname\"; use `10.0.20.112`).\n- **PITFALL: credential-extraction regexes coupling scripts to memory formatting.**\n  ssh_run-style scripts that parse the profile MEMORY.md for the password (`r\"root/(\\S+?)\\)\"`\n  etc.) break when memory entries are rewritten (a batch prune changed one entry's\n  phrasing and every helper died with `AssertionError: credential not found`). Make the\n  regex resilient (match `root/<word>` anywhere: `r\"root/(\\w+)\"`), or read the credential\n  from a dedicated file the scripts own — and after any memory rewrite, smoke-test one\n  helper before batch-driving the fleet.\n- **Never treat a `[Pp]assword` match after login as auth rejection** — `qm config`\n  prints a literal `cipassword: ****` line and VM login loops print `Password:`; check for\n  `Permission denied` instead, or you'll report false auth failures.\n- Host SSH rate-limits rapid reconnects → pace calls, batch commands into one base64'd\n  script run once.\n- **PITFALL: pexpect `child.before` empty/truncated on long batched output.** A single\n  `expect(EOF)` + one `child.before` read can return EMPTY (match at buffer start) or\n  lose most output. Drain instead: loop\n  `expect([r'[Pp]assword:', pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT])` until EOF, appending\n  `child.before` on every iteration, then `\"\".join()` the pieces. Keep the password\n  pattern inside the loop so late prompts still match; treat success as the absence of\n  `Permission denied` in the joined text.\n- Large binaries to offline hosts: serve via `python3 -m http.server` on the internet-\n  capable LAN host, `curl` inside the Proxmox host. pexpect-scp with `encoding='utf-8'`\n  corrupts binaries.\n\n### `qm guest exec` / `pct exec` / `pct push` only work on the OWNING node\n\n`/etc/pve` is cluster-readable, but guest-exec commands resolve the guest config against\nthe LOCAL node. Running `qm guest exec 103` from a node that doesn't own VM103 fails with\n`Configuration file 'nodes/<wrong-node>/qemu-server/103.conf' does not exist`. SSH to the\nowning node first (fleet map: VM103→miam-00111, VM401→miam-00112, VM109→miam-00143,\nVM111→miam-00144; CT115→miam-00147). `pct push` additionally requires the CT to be running.\n\n**PITFALL: `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -s` does NOT forward stdin.** A heredoc piped at\n`qm guest exec` feeds the node-side qm process, not the guest — the call returns\n`{\"exitcode\":0}` with EMPTY `out-data` while the guest script never ran. This silently\nno-ops entire batch installs. The reliable pattern is base64-in-command:\n`qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -c \"echo <base64> | base64 -d | bash\"`.\nSame class of confusion: `scp node:/tmp/x` lands the file on the NODE — to move a file\nnode→CT use `pct push <ctid> /tmp/x /tmp/x` (and `pct pull` to fetch out).\n\n### Inspecting the GUEST vs the NODE (verify your vantage point)\n\nA script run directly through SSH lands on the NODE shell — its output (`pve-root` df,\nQEMU process list, host `lspci`, missing `nvidia-smi`) describes the hypervisor, NOT the\nguest. This burned a full diagnostic round: a \"bare guest\" verdict was drawn from node-shell\noutput while the guest was actually fully built (drivers + venv + model cache intact).\nRule: every guest-internal probe (cloud-init status, nvidia-smi, venv/model dirs, guest FS\nusage, guest internet) MUST be wrapped in `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -lc '...'` on the\nowning node. Before mutating anything based on an inspection, state which vantage point\nproduced the evidence.\n\n### Deploying a file INTO a VM guest when your box has no direct key\n\n1. base64 the deploy script locally; run it to the owning node over SSH, decode to /tmp there.\n2. From the node: `scp /tmp/x.sh <guest-user>@<guest-ip>:/tmp/` — the node holds the SSH\n   keypair baked into the guest's cloud-init `authorized_keys` (e.g. the `root@miam-00135`\n   RSA key referenced in cloud-config users).\n3. From the node: `ssh <guest-user>@<guest-ip> \"sudo bash /tmp/deploy.sh\"` — the cloud-init\n   user has NOPASSWD sudo. Direct SSH from your own box only works if YOUR key is in the\n   guest's authorized_keys; otherwise this node-relay path is the reliable route.\n\n### Debian 12 LXCs ship no sudo\n\n`sudo: command not found` inside a fresh Debian 12 CT. As root use\n`runuser -u <user> -- <cmd>` (e.g. `runuser -u postgres -- psql ...`). Locale/UTF-8 perl\nwarnings in these CTs are harmless noise.\n\n### Reconstructing an undocumented service into systemd from bash_history\n\nWhen a guest runs a critical service (e.g. vLLM) with no unit file, recover the final\nbattle-tested invocation from `grep -ihE '<service>' /root/.bash_history /home/*/.bash_history`\n—the LAST occurrence is the current working config. Turn it into\n`/etc/systemd/system/<svc>.service` (Environment lines for exports like HF_HOME, ExecStart\nwith all flags, Restart=on-failure, TimeoutStartSec generous for model load), then\n`daemon-reload && enable --now`, and verify via the service's HTTP endpoint\n(`/v1/models` + a small chat completion for vLLM) before calling it up.\n- Read a headless guest's console via `socat - UNIX-CONNECT:/var/run/qemu-server/<vmid>.serial0`\n  under `pty.fork()` — the only view before the guest agent/network are up. Verified\n  working pattern: `pty.fork()` + `os.execvp('socat', ...)`, write `\\n`, then\n  `select()`-loop reads for ~20s; a booting/idle guest yields the `login:` banner.\n  Wrap it in a script landed on the node via base64-heredoc, then `python3 <script>`\n  — nodes have no pexpect installed (stdlib `pty`/`select` only).\n- **cloud-init on the Ubuntu noble image applies network/creds ONLY on first boot.**\n  Bake `ipconfig0/ciuser/cipassword/sshkeys` BEFORE `qm start` on a freshly created VM.\n  Patching a booted VM then `qm reboot` does NOT re-apply them.\n  ### Re-run WITHOUT recreating: bump the cloud-init instance-id\n  When a first boot half-applied (ssh/packages missing, wrong IP) you do NOT need to\n  destroy/rebuild: edit the user snippet (`/var/lib/vz/snippets/<name>.yaml`), bump\n  `instance-id` in the meta snippet (e.g. `bootstrap-v2` → `v3`), set static IP via\n  `qm set <vmid> --ipconfig0 ip=<ip>/24,gw=<gw>`, then `qm stop <vmid> && qm start <vmid>`\n  (start regenerates the cloud-init ISO; the instance-id change forces cloud-init to\n  re-run per-instance modules). Poll readiness with `timeout 2 bash -c 'echo > /dev/tcp/<ip>/22'`\n  + `qm agent <vmid> ping`, then verify `cloud-init status --long` = done/no errors.\n  ### Static vs DHCP on vmbr0: VERIFY before asserting\n  Do NOT assume the LAN lacks DHCP — verified 2026-09 this vmbr0 /24 HAS a DHCP\n  server (a cloud-init `ip=dhcp` VM leased an address; all Hermes LXCs run `ip=dhcp`).\n  **After any build assigns statics, run the full audit in\n  `references/network-ip-collision-audit.md`** (asset-sheet MAC cross-check + ARP\n  classification + OPNsense DHCP pool boundary check via config.xml download) — the\n  user explicitly asks for this double-check, and it caught the corosync collision\n  below plus stale .115 references left in app configs.\n  Still assign STATIC IPs to infrastructure roles (managers/gateways/DB) so identities\n  never drift, but prove an address is free before assigning:\n  1) `qm list`/`pct list` per node + `pvesh get /cluster/nextid`\n  2) `grep -rE '10\\.0\\.20\\.[0-9]+' /etc/pve/qemu-server/ /etc/pve/lxc/`\n  3) `ip neigh show` + parallel ping sweep of the /24\n  4) **CLUSTER-WIDE: also check `/etc/pve/corosync.conf` `ring0_addr` values** — bare-metal\n  cluster nodes own

### [2026-09-21T20:38:46.083653+00:00] assistant

Plan executed end-to-end, autonomously. Cluster is quorate with all 16 nodes online and every model endpoint serving.

## Maintenance complete — MARION-IA-USA

**Updates applied:** 16/16 nodes, **~1,838 packages**, `apt_rc=0`, **zero removals, zero errors, `dpkg --audit` clean** everywhere. Every transaction was `--simulate`-reviewed first; kernel / firmware / NVIDIA / RDMA / PBS / MTU / hostname all untouched, and the **running kernel was identical before and after every reboot**.

**Reboots:** 13 nodes, one at a time, guests gracefully stopped → verified → restarted.
- Rebooted: 00110, 00113, 00114, 00115, 00117, 00118, 00119, 00147, 00149, 00111, 00112, 00143, 00144
- **Not rebooted:** `miam-00100` (plan forbids it — manual instructions in the handoff) and **`miam-00133` / `miam-00135`**, which I deliberately left alone: they host LLDAP, PDM, the tailscale router, the LLM Manager and the other Hermes agent VMs, and a standing hard boundary forbids restarting them. They're fully updated, so this is a zero-urgency human decision.

**The two findings that matter:**

| Node | Finding | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **00112** | Corrected ECC errors on **`DIMM_P0_H0`** (EDAC mc0 channel 7), ~1 per 8 min, same address window — and **they came back after a full reboot** (3 CEs in the first 24 min), `ue_count = 0` | **HARDWARE SERVICE REQUIRED** — reseat/replace that RDIMM, inspect the bent socket pins on channel 7. Not yet "remove from production". |
| **00111** | Corrected PCIe AER storm on root port `0000:00:01.1` (BadTLP/BadDLLP): 22,758 corrected pre-reboot, 88 more within 20 min post-reboot, **zero uncorrectable/fatal** | MONITOR + physical riser/cable/slot inspection — deferred to the PCIe/RDMA project |

Also found (and **not** changed): VM109 (`.163`) runs **two enabled units with an identical `vllm serve` ExecStart** — `vllm.service` works, `vllm-qwen.service` has been failing on every boot since Sep 18. That predates this run and is worth consolidating.

**Two things worth flagging honestly:** during 00111's reboot a transient PVE-API read timeout aborted my poll loop — the node completed its boot on its own and I re-verified it in `verifyonly` mode (no second reboot); I hardened the driver afterwards. And `.163`/`.164` looked "down" for ~10 min after their hosts rebooted — that was just vLLM reloading, confirmed by checking inside the guests before acting.
